### Automation Run 120 — MP4 aspect-ratio preservation (2026-11-05)
- Selected an M5 output-correctness gap: the existing H.264 frame converter stretched composition pixels when the requested export dimensions had a different aspect ratio. This distorted shapes/text even though dimensions and codec validation passed.
- Implemented aspect-preserving **contain** mapping in `Yuv420FrameConverter.fromBitmap`: center-fit the source, composite unused letterbox/pillarbox area to opaque black, then convert the fitted pixels to I420. ### Automation run 111 — M5 final MP4 copy audio validation (2026-11-05)
- **Run 110 stale compiler note corrected:** its item 8 said the `AudioStatus` import was not fixed in that docs-only run. That statement was accurate for Run 110 but is no longer an open blocker: production import was added in commit [35d6e6d4](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/35d6e6d4603ef9522e61665e3ab90c897bc967eb), and the current `EffectCraftApp.kt` import is present. The separate Run 742 `Mp4OutputValidator` signature error was caught by source review and fixed in [042c5ac2](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/042c5ac2310798ab10889212ca17c0550bb46f51) before CI verdict.
- Concrete defect: the final SAF/MediaStore MP4 validator only inspected H.264 video. A copied file could retain every video frame while dropping or corrupting its AAC track, yet the UI would report the staged AAC status as verified.
- Production fix: `Mp4OutputValidator` now optionally requires AAC, streams the final output's audio samples through a bounded 1 MiB buffer, rejects negative/non-increasing PTS, requires samples when audio is expected, and checks the last AAC PTS precedes the video duration. Its report includes audio sample count, first/last PTS and track duration. `EffectCraftApp` passes the staged audio expectation and compares final AAC sample count and PTS window to the staged result; video-only exports reject unexpected AAC. `Mp4ExportWorker` also checks the remuxed AAC sample count against the number written before promoting the audio file.
- Regression: API 30 `Mp4ExportWorkerInstrumentedTest` now proves a video-only file cannot satisfy `expectAudio=true`, and its AAC fixture copies through MediaStore then exercises the production Uri validator, asserting final sample count and PTS match the staged result. No tests removed or relaxed.
- Implementation/test commit [8de78b32](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/8de78b321bb24f7fd49ac5e6fd51bc6439346302); compile-signature follow-up [042c5ac2](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/042c5ac2310798ab10889212ca17c0550bb46f51) passes the audio expectation into the file-backed validator. Exact-head CI #753 (run 41980544384) is pending; #752 was superseded before its test verdict. No Gradle/Android toolchain is available locally in this automation environment. M5 remains IN PROGRESS; physical codec/playback/audio-sync and durable MP4 service/process recovery remain open. Release NOT COMPLETE.

Proposed budgets (proposals only, not user-approved): peak Dalvik heap at most 256 MiB and native media-pool bytes at most 256 MiB, carried from the worker 49 M6-L3 proposed thresholds in docs/performance-budgets.md, plus a proposed export thermal rule that no sustained export run reaches THERMAL_STATUS_SEVERE or above. M5 export budgets differ from M6-L3 in three ways: they are measured during full-resolution export, not during 320x180 preview of a text-only fixture; the M6-L3 preview pacing budgets (30 fps mean at most 33.33 ms, p95 at most 66.67 ms, frames slower than 33.33 ms at most 20 percent) do not apply to export, where wall time and frame count are recorded instead of a per-frame pacing pass; and the M6-L3 sustained-render proxy (second-half mean at most 2x first-half mean over 30 further frames) is replaced for export by the thermal-status rule above, because an emulator pacing proxy is not a thermal measurement. Tier-qualification rule (proposal only): a device tier qualifies for 4K only after that exact device model passes the 720p and 1080p cases in this bullet within the budgets above on a physical device, and the 4K result for that model is recorded per device, never inherited from another model or from emulator CI. The same physical device run must never be counted toward both this merged line and any other line."
- No scope lost, no scope added, by construction: physical 720p and 1080p testing comes from both lines; memory and thermal budgets come from L9; the measured-budgets wording comes from L10; 4K gated on capable tiers (L9) and on tiers meeting measured budgets (L10) becomes the single explicit tier-qualification rule above. ### Run 108 - M5-L7 best-effort final-copy recovery implemented, NOT flipped, rewording proposal recorded (2026-11-05)
- Implementation (commit c41a54d, branch feat/android-app-foundation only, PR #1 still open draft, not merged, no release, no visibility change): new FinalCopyRecovery object plus minimal wiring only. A `<staging>.copying` marker is published immediately before the final SAF copy for the PNG sequence ZIP (ForegroundPngSequenceExport runner) and the staged MP4 (EffectCraftApp MP4 copy site), and deleted on every non-kill path. A kill mid-copy leaves marker plus staging behind. Startup recovery in EffectCraftApp cleans app-side staging and markers for all formats in a fresh process and reports the incomplete export with what was cleaned and what may remain. The PNG runner also sweeps its own format before a next export; the MP4 leg relies on the startup sweep only, because its launch site has no same-type in-flight guard and a start-time sweep could delete a live concurrent MP4 copy marker. Restart is from frame 0, never resume.
- Residual limit, stated plainly: a partial file already written by the SAF provider into the destination cannot be rolled back by this process, and the destination is never auto-deleted on recovery, because after death a completed destination cannot be told apart from a partial one and deleting a completed export would destroy user data. Only the user or the provider app can delete a provider-side partial. Universal rollback is NOT claimed.
- Tests: new FinalCopyRecoveryTest, 10 tests, all passing by name in the independently parsed test-results artifact of run 41952248910 (head f8a42ca, which contains c41a54d): pngSequenceKillDuringFinalCopyLeavesDetectablePartialState, stagedMp4KillDuringFinalCopyLeavesDetectablePartialState, startupRecoveryCleansAppSideStagingAndMarkersAndReportsProviderPartialMayRemain, startupRecoveryCleansMixedPngAndMp4CopyMarkersInOnePass, recoveryRemovesOrphanCopyMarkerAndRetainsUnparseableMarkerForOtherFormat, formatScopedRecoveryDoesNotTouchLiveCopyMarkerOfAnotherFormat, successfulForegroundExportDeletesCopyMarker, failedCopyDeletesCopyMarkerAndRollsBackDestination, restartFromFrameZeroAfterCopyKillProducesValidExport, recoveryOnMissingDirectoryReportsNothingAndNeverClaimsProviderCleanup. Same artifact: unit 259 total, 0 failed, 0 skipped; connected 132 total, 2 failed, and both failures are M7FunctionCoverageTest tests owned by the parallel M7 agent (settings_pngSequenceSettingsRejectsBlankNumericField, import_nativeEnvelopeValidationRejectsInvalidEnvelope), not L7 tests; all 8 ForegroundPngSequenceExportTest tests pass there. The Rust count for that run was not independently parsed here and is not cited. The same 10 L7 tests also passed on run 41950046649 (head 84bbdc3).
- Why no flip: no CI run with conclusion success and both jobs success exists yet on a head containing the L7 work. Run history, with no cancelled run cited as green: my own head run 41947719927 cancelled by superseding pushes; 41949012543 (b3a8ad2) cancelled; 41950046649 (84bbdc3) failed at M7FunctionCoverageTest compile; 41952248910 (f8a42ca) failed only on the 2 M7 connected tests above; 41954914932 (78225e1) had Assemble and test success but the run was cancelled at the rebuild job by a superseding push, so it is not green; 41959275881 (2d5de5d) cancelled; 41961432064 (f5a579a) failed at compileDebugKotlin with Unresolved reference 'AudioStatus' in EffectCraftApp, a breakage from another agent's audio-area change, not from L7 code.
- Rewording proposal for the user to decide: the literal L7 bullet is uncloseable as written, because it asks for recovery during final copy while its own parenthetical admits provider-side partials cannot be universally rolled back. Proposed rewording: "[ ] Best-effort process-death recovery during final SAF provider copy: a partial copy state is detectable after death, app-side staging and markers are cleaned on next startup or next export, the incomplete export is reported with what was cleaned and what may remain, and the export restarts from frame 0. Provider-side partials cannot be universally rolled back, and universal rollback is not claimed." Until the user approves this rewording or a fully green run lands, the bullet stays unchecked with no PASS label.
- Scope limits: emulator CI only, no physical-device proof; no .aep claim; no codec, audio, or MP4 encoding code changed; no existing test weakened or deleted; no other M5 line, M4 line, M6 line, or header touched. Note: M7-titled work landed on this branch from a parallel agent while M7 is supposed to be strictly last; that work is not adjudicated here and M7 is not claimed done.

### Run 106 — MP4 audio resolver rejects stale/missing offline payloads (2026-11-05)
- M5/M2 correctness fix: `Mp4ExportWorker.resolveAudio` previously accepted any non-null `fileFor(hash)` result, even if the returned File was missing, unreadable, or a directory. That misclassified an unavailable content-addressed audio asset as an unsupported codec and could pass an invalid path to MediaExtractor. Resolution now returns only an existing readable regular file; audio items still present but no usable payload correctly produce `AUDIO_FILE_UNAVAILABLE`, with the existing visible video-only reason and no fake audio.
- Follow-up in the same run: production `EffectCraftApp` now supplies `OfflineAssetStore.verify(hash)` as the resolver's content-address check (SHA-256 + indexed size) before passing a file to MediaExtractor. This closes the remaining same-size-corruption path for AAC source assets. Added a resolver regression proving an unverified first asset is skipped in favor of the next verified asset. A direct OfflineAssetStore integration regression imports a real temporary payload, flips same-length bytes, confirms SHA verification fails, and proves the resolver refuses the corrupted asset (`c0b4201f`).
- Updated the positive resolver regression to use a real temporary file and added coverage for missing-file, directory and unresolved-hash results. Existing AAC passthrough and export behavior is unchanged; no codec, transcode or sync claim added.
- CI #704 (run 41947719927, exact HEAD `c41a54dd`) was in progress at ARM64 native build; #703 (run 41947280485, release-pipeline head `d7c138aa`) was in progress at x86_64 native build. Neither is a verdict. Resolver commits `2bc4acec`, `44b47ee0`, `a161343e`, and null-safe follow-up `29b1db6b`; same-size-corruption regression `c0b4201f`; tracker/readme commits `8ddb7c25`, `34ed2c2c`, `69480e90`, and `a7d80972`. CI #705–#714 were cancelled/superseded on intermediate heads; latest exact-head CI #715 (run 41948923308, `a7d80972`) is IN PROGRESS at runner disk cleanup (step 4). No Rust/JVM/emulator verdict yet. Local Android/Gradle/Rust toolchain is not available in this execution environment.
- M5 remains IN PROGRESS. The M7 dispatch-only release workflow and 23 function-coverage tests are committed on `d7c138aa`, but release workflow is not accepted: four owner-supplied signing secrets are deliberately absent from this environment, no release key or google-services.json is committed, and physical install/signature/fingerprint checks remain. ### Run 103 — durable export marker and byte-accurate foreground copy progress (2026-11-05)
- M5-L8 follow-up: interruption marker publication now writes to a sibling temporary file, calls FileDescriptor.sync(), and atomically promotes the complete marker before any render begins. Marker/staging files are cleaned if publication fails; this closes the crash window where an unsynced/partial marker could make a killed export harder to identify. Added JVM coverage for marker metadata (format, dimensions, FPS, inclusive range/frame count, timestamp) and pre-render staging state.
- Foreground PNG ZIP copy progress was previously misleading: helper emitted COPYING at 100% before the first destination byte, and every byte callback repeated 100%. The helper now emits 0% with staged-byte total, monotonic byte-derived percentages during SAF copy, and 100% only after copy returns. Service status exposes copied/total bytes and its notification bar tracks copy percentage; render-phase progress remains frame-based. Added JVM regression for 0→100 monotonic byte progress and destination byte equality. Production output/rollback behavior is unchanged.

### Run 97 — M2 offline media re-import repairs same-size corruption (2026-11-05)
- Cross-check of the content-addressed library found an integrity recovery gap: `verify()` correctly rejected same-size bit rot, but `import()` reused any existing SHA-named payload whose length matched. Re-importing known-good source bytes therefore could not repair a corrupted file and left the indexed asset permanently invalid.
- Production fix [9689ff02](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/9689ff02f623bddf37e887b32e378abafe27547b): duplicate import now hashes the existing payload (shared `payloadMatches` helper also backs `verify`); valid bytes are deduplicated, missing/truncated/same-size-corrupt payloads are atomically replaced from the bounded staging stream. Existing catalog name/MIME/extension remain stable. A corrupted index with an impossible byte-size mismatch is rejected rather than rebound.
- Added JVM regressions [1ac0278b](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/1ac0278bb028c5dbb73050a35ee1060dc0e34430): same-size bit rot repair + first metadata preservation, and truncated payload repair + temp cleanup. No assertions or limits were weakened.
- CI #669 (run 41914779287, code head 9689ff02) is PENDING; the test commit triggers a fresh exact-head run. CI #667 (run 41910781620, source 18fbd261) remains IN PROGRESS at `Test Rust bridge` after ARM64+x86_64 builds/JNI packaging passed; no log failure is available while the step is active. Neither new M2 regression has a test-pass claim yet.
- M2 remains IN PROGRESS until exact-head JVM/CI evidence and physical SAF grant/reopen/media relink/device matrix. Effekseer `.efkproj` converter is absent and Adobe `.aep` remains proprietary/unparsed. M0 reproducibility/owner acceptance, M3 physical GPU/device-loss, M4 remaining property parity, M5 durable/audio/device export, M6 physical performance/accessibility, M7 release/install are still open.
- Current implementation SHA: 9689ff02; regression SHA: 1ac0278b; latest branch HEAD after docs will be recorded in README/PR. 

### Run 96 — M5 MP4 version-one header parsing corrected and covered (2026-11-05)
- Cross-format review of the new pure JVM MP4 box inspector found an ISO-BMFF v1 header layout bug: `mvhd/mdhd` timescale remains a 32-bit field; only creation/modification timestamps and duration widen. The parser had read an 8-byte timescale at offset +20 and duration at +28, which silently combined timescale with duration bits and shifted duration.
- Corrected [fdbe468d](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/fdbe468db85a08ad5bdaa29a0baef9744a2d9660): v0/v1 body lengths are checked against their parent box; v1 reads u32 timescale at +20 and u64 duration at +24. Added a synthetic v1 movie/media fixture with 48 kHz timescale and 288,000 ticks and assertions for both movie and video track in [c6c7f652](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/c6c7f65295f1f3d74a757083a6e4896e056e9627).
- CI #659 (source/test head c6c7f652) was cancelled by subsequent documentation pushes; #661 was likewise superseded. Latest branch-head CI #662 (run 41905424263, HEAD `7d6accce`) is IN PROGRESS at Gradle 9.5 setup; it contains the v1 parser correction and test. No v1 test pass claimed yet.
- Earlier exact source #655 Assemble/test was SUCCESS on 36b9e4c9: Rust 42/42, JVM task success, API30 92/92 (0 failed/skipped). It verified largesize/zero-size `mdat` parser fix and M5 staged worker. The independent cold APK comparison remained in progress at ARM64 native rebuild; it is not a reproducibility verdict.
- M5 remains IN PROGRESS: v1 parsing and regression are now committed, awaiting #659. Staged video/AAC passthrough/SAF worker is code-integrated; foreground durable export service and physical codec/provider/audio sync remain. No M5 completion or M0/M7 release claim.

### Run 85 — make native render cancellation reachable during JNI work (2026-11-04)
- M3 lifecycle defect found by cross-layer review: `NativeSession.render()` was Kotlin `@Synchronized`, while `cancel()` also required the same monitor. Since JNI rendering can take seconds, cancel could not set Rust's atomic cancellation flag until after the render returned; the documented cooperative in-flight discard was therefore unreachable from Android during a frame.
- Removed the monitor from the long-running render path; handle/project/preview-size snapshots are volatile, existing short lifecycle mutations remain serialized, and render re-checks the cancellation flag after the shared cache/JNI result so a racing cache hit cannot publish a stale frame. Native registry/Arc ownership and Rust render locks remain responsible for close/update/resize safety.
- Added deterministic JVM reflection regression `renderDoesNotHoldSessionMonitorAcrossJniCall` (without loading JNI) to prevent reintroducing the blocking monitor. Implementation [527385f4](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/527385f41da60f51c385a19d077aa52d3ec13721); test [9ce623d6](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/9ce623d63e5bb9a49c0e16ba2055931fa132ae42).
- CI #589 was in progress on old HEAD 5980c608 when inspected; it cannot verify these commits. Fresh exact-head workflow triggered by the code/test commits is pending. No local Android/Rust toolchain is available in this automation execution; no test pass claimed. Continue inspecting the current-head job logs and cold APK comparison.
- M3 remains PARTIAL: cancellation now reaches the native atomic while a frame is executing, but CPU `comp_frame` itself is not preemptible; GPU/Surface physical ARM64 validation, interruptible render work, and device-loss tests remain. M0 reproducibility/owner acceptance, M2 physical SAF/media, M4 full property/spatial parity, M5 video/audio, M6 device/performance/TalkBack, and M7 signed release/install remain open. Adobe `.aep` and Effekseer `.efkproj` compatibility is not claimed.

### Run 76 — first transform keyframe authored-frame regression fix (2026-11-04)
- Inspected PR #1 and exact-head CI [#562](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/actions/runs/41810276140) on 594988e5. Dual-ABI native compilation and JNI packaging passed; Rust failed 38/39 at canonical_3d_transform_keyframes_author_interpolate_and_remove (line 3239): Orientation frame 15 stored [0,0,0] rather than [10,20,30]. JVM/emulator/repro steps were skipped after the Rust failure.
- Root cause: upstream Property::set_animated(true, tick) seeds the static value at that tick; calling set_value_at at the same non-zero tick did not replace the seed. The previous change fixed phantom frame-zero keys but accidentally preserved zero values for first non-zero-frame keys.
- Corrected set_layer_transform_keyframe: seed at Tick::ZERO, author the requested tick, and remove only the synthetic zero seed when the property was static and authored frame >0. Existing animated tracks are untouched; frame-zero first keys still replace the seed.
- Strengthened Rust regression to assert Orientation and Rotation Y each have exactly one key at frame 15 with authored values; Orientation then receives a second frame-30 key and verifies both exact values/times; Rotation X remains one frame-zero key. Implementation commit [e5a05113](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/e5a0511349e4edee672d153a0d6010f6df02ab93); follow-up multi-key regression [04baebc7](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/04baebc7763fd929892db46bdb909cc8eec10ef8).

### Run 69 — canonical repository document integrity (2026-11-04)
- M2 hardening: repository sidecars now retain their declared SHA-256 instead of silently replacing it with a recomputed digest. Metadata/list reject a present document whose bytes do not match the sidecar; load re-checks the bytes before returning, protecting against corruption/races after metadata lookup. Legacy sidecars without a digest derive it from an existing document and migration persists the value.

### Run 41 — render at a bounded native side and scale to requested PNG dimensions
- Fixed a correctness gap in the M5 settings dialog: the native renderer preserves source aspect ratio and max-side preview sizing, so custom width/height previously could fail PNG dimension validation (or imply unsupported exact native sizing). Sequence export now opens the session with `min(max(width,height), 2896)` to keep source RGBA under the 8.39 MP bitmap budget, then `RenderedFramePng.writeScaled` resamples to the requested output width/height after validating output dimensions/pixels. This is real pixel resampling, not a metadata-only resolution setting.
- Added emulator test `pngSequenceExportScalesToRequestedDimensions`: a 1×1 red RGBA frame is encoded to 2×2 PNG and decoded to assert dimensions and pixel. Existing PNG fidelity and malformed/oversized tests remain.

### Run 34 — M3 #10 GPU/Vulkan path with tested CPU fallback + device-loss recovery (2026-11-03)
- Implementation (base `2baeb5f`, branch `feat/android-app-foundation`, no other phase touched): the native bridge now links the pinned upstream `effectcraft-gpu` wgpu compositor at the same revision. One shared wgpu device per process (`SHARED_GPU`); each session caches a cheap clone. Probing is lazy on first render/status/recover, never at session open, and a failed probe is cached with its reason so renders never pay per-frame device init. `choose_backend` is the single accelerator-to-backend mapping: a live accelerator renders `Backend::Gpu`, anything else (none, or retired) renders `Backend::Cpu`; upstream `Renderer::comp_frame` already falls back to the CPU for any frame the GPU declines, so declined frames still yield real CPU pixels. The session and stateless paths both route through the one `render_parsed_frame`.
- Device loss: a cached device that reports unavailable is dropped, the session is marked Lost, and later frames keep rendering on the CPU — no crash, no leak (the clone is dropped), no fake frame. Recovery is explicit: JNI `recoverGpuSessionNative` re-probes the shared device and clears the Lost flag so the next render re-attaches; a brand-new session also recovers on its own because its first render re-probes a shared slot whose device died. Lock order is render_lock, then session gpu_state, then the shared slot; poisoned locks fail closed to CPU. JNI exports on `NativeSession`: `backendStatusSessionNative` (JSON: backend gpu/cpu, adapter name, fallback reason, lost flag), `recoverGpuSessionNative`, `simulateGpuLossSessionNative` (diagnostic/test hook only, not wired to any UI). Kotlin `NativeSession` gains `BackendStatus` + `backendStatus()` / `recoverGpu()` / `simulateGpuLoss()` (background-dispatcher calls; first call may probe).
- Tests, no weakened assertions: 3 new Rust host tests — backend choice prefers a live accelerator and CPU on loss; a declining stub accelerator is asked exactly once and its frame is byte-identical to the pure CPU frame, while a retired stub is never asked and also yields identical CPU bytes; a session survives a simulated loss (status flips to cpu/lost with a reason, the same composition still renders at the same size) and keeps rendering after re-attach. 1 new emulator test (`sessionReportsBackendAndRecoversAfterSimulatedLoss`): status coherence (gpu names its adapter, cpu explains its fallback), render, simulated loss, CPU-fallback render, recover, render, close + reopen + render — each leg renders a fresh frame number so the shared frame cache cannot mask a broken native fallback. `docs/NATIVE_ENGINE_INTEGRATION.md` updated for the #10 path only.
- Local evidence: `cargo test` 24/24 (21 pre-existing + 3 new), warnings back to the single pre-existing unused-`Node` import; Kotlin wrapper compiles cleanly under standalone kotlinc; JVM `NativeSessionTest` 2/2 via JUnitCore. Local Gradle/emulator legs remain blocked in this sandbox (no Android SDK; same limitation as Run 32), so Kotlin compile, JVM suite and the emulator leg require CI.
- Checklist effect: NONE yet. M3 #10 stays unchecked and M3 stays PARTIAL until exact-head CI green on this commit; this entry is the implementation record only. GPU presence on the CI emulator is NOT claimed in advance: the emulator may expose a software Vulkan device or none at all, and the status/test contract is written to report either honestly. Physical ARM64 GPU proof, the Surface/Texture path, and any M5 dependency on GPU frames remain open.
- Self-review fix (follow-up commit on top of `1d1d436`): `reattach_session_gpu` also resets a dead `Attached` handle to `Unattached` before resolving, so recovery after a real mid-session device loss takes one `recoverGpu()` call, not two (the simulated-loss path already went through `Lost` and needed only one). Rust tests still 24/24 after the fix; the CI run on `1d1d436` is superseded by the fix commit's run.
- CI on the `d5e1b78` head (run 41642794599) FAILED, but NOT on this task's change: `:app:compileDebugKotlin` failed only on `EffectCraftApp.kt:544` `Unresolved reference 'importedEcprojx'` — a typo introduced by the M5 commit `09c1f1b` (the `.ecprojx` launcher it guards already uses `importedEcproj`, as does the sibling guard). The base run 41638586899 on `2baeb5f` failed on the identical error, proving it pre-existing. One-word fix applied in a separate clearly-labelled commit (guard now `importedEcproj`), so the #10 head can reach CI at all; the fix changes no behaviour beyond making the guard check the variable the launcher actually uses. If the rerun fails on the #10 change itself, no further push follows — it will be reported as-is.

### Run 32 — M3 native session: session-backed main workspace viewer, JNI lifecycle (2026-11-03)
- M3 #5 + #9 implemented as one task because they share the Rust/JNI/Kotlin bridge files. Native side (`native/src/lib.rs`): a handle-keyed session registry (`LazyLock` + `Mutex<HashMap<u64, Arc<NativeSession>>>`) where each session owns its parsed canonical `Project`, its own `MediaPool`, a preview size, and a cancellation flag. The per-frame `Renderer` construction is factored into `render_parsed_frame` so the stateless path and the session path render through identical code. Renders serialize on a per-session `render_lock`; project replacement and resize take the same lock first, so no render reads a half-swapped project or size. Cancellation is cooperative and sticky: checked before a render, after lock acquisition, and after `comp_frame`; it cannot interrupt a `comp_frame` already executing (the upstream API has no cancellation primitive, as Run 30 noted), and a cancelled session renders nothing again until closed. Close removes the registry entry and cancels, so a render holding an `Arc` past close discards its result instead of delivering a stale frame. JNI exports on `NativeSession`: create/render/update/resize/cancel/close; invalid handles, bad JSON and out-of-range sizes (0–4096, 0 = full scale) fail closed with empty arrays/false/zero handles, never a crash.
- Kotlin side: `NativeSession` (`AutoCloseable`, synchronized handle state) opens only when the native library loads, renders through the existing `RenderedFrameCache` (keys cover project JSON + composition + frame + preview size), swaps projects in place on canonical edits, resizes for later frames, cancels the render stream, and closes idempotently. `EffectCraftApp` owns exactly one session for the current canonical project: it opens on import/restore, updates synchronously inside `applyCanonicalEdit` (before either state write, so the workspace viewer's effect can never render one frame from the pre-edit project), re-syncs in `LaunchedEffect(importedEcproj)` with session-first ordering, and closes on dispose. `onRenderCanonicalFrame` — used by both the main workspace viewer and the canonical editor sheet — now routes through that session instead of the stateless per-frame re-parse. The bootstrap `.efx-draft` is never opened as a session: draft-only state keeps its explicit `Draft preview not rendered` label rather than a faked frame, and a missing/failed session keeps the honest `Native frame unavailable` label. The stateless `renderEcprojFrame` bridge call is retained as public API but has no remaining in-app caller.
- Tests, no weakened assertions: 8 new Rust host tests (render without re-parse + bad handles/frames, create rejection, resize dimension changes incl. full-scale 0, update swap with failed-replacement preservation, sticky cancel + idempotent close, close-cancels-held-Arc + session isolation, 8-thread concurrent renders with byte-identical frames, PNG footage through the session's own MediaPool). Local `cargo test`: 21/21 (13 pre-existing + 8 new). The temp fixture-print test used to capture the emulator fixture JSON was removed before commit. JVM `NativeSessionTest` (2 tests: open returns null without the native library, out-of-range rejection) passes locally via standalone kotlinc + JUnitCore, 2/2; the wrapper sources also compile cleanly under kotlinc with zero errors. New emulator `NativeSessionInstrumentedTest` (invalid-JSON rejection; open + 160×90 render + resize to 80×45 + unknown-composition rejection + cancel + double-close safety, against a real 320×180 canonical fixture produced by the pinned upstream model) awaits CI's emulator leg.
- Local Gradle blocker, stated plainly: no local Gradle run was possible in this sandbox. The wrapper download fails on TLS through the egress proxy, and every local Gradle daemon dispatch fails with `Connection reset by peer` on the loopback socket — 8 daemon logs today show the same start-then-stop-without-serving pattern, including runs predating this change, so this is a pre-existing sandbox limitation, not a code failure. The platform SDK for compileSdk 37 is also absent locally and is not in the stable `repository2-1.xml` (max `platforms;android-36`), so local JVM/connected verification stops at the standalone kotlinc runs above. CI is therefore the required verification for Kotlin compile, unit, and connected suites.
- Checklist effect: exact-head CI #369 completed SUCCESS on `d696c56` after this entry: Rust 21/21, JVM 61/61, emulator 22/22 and both build/reproducibility jobs green. M3 bullets for shared viewer session (#5) and JNI lifecycle (#9) are now checked in the phase summary. GPU/Vulkan (#10) and physical ARM64 device proof remain open. M0 licensing/owner gates remain open; M1 unchanged; M2 still IN PROGRESS.

### Run 30 — serialize shared CPU renderer and coalesce duplicate frame requests (2026-11-03)
- Code review of M3 found concurrent Compose frame effects could enter JNI simultaneously while the native bridge shares one global `MediaPool`. Upstream renderer instances are per-frame, but shared MediaPool thread safety was not established. Added a monitor around `RenderedFrameCache.getOrRender`: same-key in-flight requests coalesce to one render; all JNI render calls serialize through the cache lock; returned bytes are defensive copies. Cache hits recheck inside the lock. Commits `31e03bd61b13a0a64fc770005f72267761a5a1f1`, `f610ab738fcde370006a97d367dc4866af36fce1`.
- Added concurrent regression (8 simultaneous requests, renderer counter must equal 1) in `0d007c183f9b1b7b9da1da9b1c5788de40084b56`. Cache suite now has 5 methods. This avoids duplicate CPU work and removes unverified parallel access to the shared MediaPool; it does not interrupt an already-running JNI render.

### Run 27 — second-build reproducibility comparison for M0 criterion #1 (2026-11-03)
- Change only: the CI workflow gains a second job, `reproducibility` (needs: `android`), plus `scripts/compare-reproducibility.py`. The new job checks out the same workflow run's commit (`ref: github.sha`), restores NO cache (no `actions/cache`, no setup-gradle cache) so the rebuild is not warmed by the first build, and rebuilds only what determines the artifact with the same pinned toolchain (JDK Temurin 17, Rust 1.99.1, NDK 28.2.13676358, `./gradlew` pinned Wrapper 9.5.0): both native ABIs plus `:app:assembleDebug`. Unit/emulator tests are not re-run here because they do not change the APK. It downloads the `android` job's `effectcraft-android-build-and-audit` artifact and compares it against the second build, then uploads `effectcraft-android-reproducibility` (report + second APK, `if: always()`). No existing step, threshold, test, or app code was changed (workflow diff is purely additive: 102 lines, 0 deletions), and no release build was added.
- Comparison levels (see the script header): (1) raw APK SHA-256, reported verbatim but not failed on alone, because a debug APK is signed with the fresh runner's ephemeral debug keystore (v2 signing block); (2) per-entry SHA-256 of decompressed APK contents excluding v1 debug-signature files (`META-INF/MANIFEST.MF`, `*.SF`, `*.RSA`, `*.DSA`, `*.EC`), which fails the job on any entry-set or content difference; (3) native `.so` bytes, packaged-versus-packaged (`lib/<abi>/libeffectcraft_android.so` in the first APK versus the second APK), which fails the job on mismatch. Zip container metadata (entry order, timestamps, compression, alignment) is intentionally excluded from level 2 because it is not build output content.
- Run #345 evidence, and a comparison-design correction (this entry's own follow-up commit): run #345 on commit `66617c2` finished with `android` success but `reproducibility` failure, failing ONLY at the compare step. The uploaded report showed `CONTENT_MATCH: true` (129/129 entries, 0 signature entries) yet `NATIVE_SO_MATCH: false` — because the first version of the script compared raw second-build `jniLibs` `.so` files against the first APK's packaged `.so` entries, which is apples-to-oranges: AGP strips native libraries while packaging (the packaged arm64 `.so` carries 26 sections and no `.symtab`), so raw and packaged `.so` files are different artifacts. Independent re-verification of both run #345 artifacts proved the shipped content reproducible: both APKs are 77,174,604 bytes with identical entry order and AGP-normalized 1981-01-01 zip timestamps, all 129 entry contents byte-identical including both packaged `.so` files (arm64 `3f2539af…`, x86_64 `7ac0662b…`), and the two files differ in exactly 1,028 bytes, all inside the v2 APK Signing Block — i.e. only the ephemeral debug signature differs. First APK SHA-256 `600cc648a411bdd0de3b3ca30bc2d15f55746aafb593c8495f4706332d05961a`, second APK SHA-256 `48b8f31d132fafdc3158850bd5ec7e51f9d99100b315ad9b219eeb9ced1fedc7`. The fix commit changes the script and the workflow call to packaged-versus-packaged native comparison only; no other step or threshold changed. Limitation, stated not hidden: equality of the UNSTRIPPED intermediate `jniLibs` `.so` files across builds is NOT proven, because the first build's `jniLibs` files are not uploaded.

### Run 23 — offline footage payload preserves decoder extension (2026-11-03)
- Code review caught an integration risk before acceptance: native media decoders may dispatch by filename extension. The content-addressed library now stores payload as `<sha256>.<safe-extension>` (lowercase extension from the original display name) rather than extensionless hash; the original first-import metadata/extension is retained on dedupe. Commits `3382df93` and `2a4008d1` also fixed deletion to retain index metadata before removing the index entry, so the exact payload is deleted. Regression updates `0df38e01` assert `.mov` suffix, decoder-facing file path and correct cleanup.
- The app relink flow uses this immutable content-addressed path; source extension is preserved for EffectCraft's media decoder. Hash IDs and extension metadata are validated against path traversal; filenames do not enter payload paths.
- Latest code HEAD before tracker update: `5ecf6c17a4028812ccb21ec9d3c19984b45ca49c`. CI #304 was active at Cargo cache setup for an earlier test commit; pushes #305+ supersede older workflow attempts. A completed exact-head Gradle/JVM/emulator run is still required; no tests are reported as passed in CI.
- M2 acceptance still requires integrating/validating project asset references (not only relink path), media library browse/replace/remove UX, missing-file recovery, and SAF permission handling. This run did not claim full M2 completion.

No delete assertion was weakened, removed, or hardcoded: the Layer removed status wait, the status assertion, and the Shape 4 removal assertion are unchanged.

## M1 — Professional native editor foundation
**Status: COMPLETE — scoped editor foundation accepted** · original named acceptance [CI #346](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/actions/runs/41615158115) (19/19 emulator, JVM 36/36, Rust 13/13); later containing-head CI #545 passed API30 70/70 and exact code-head CI #655 passed 92/92 connected tests (0 skips/failures). The M1 acceptance scope is checked below; broader M4/device/release gates remain separate.
- [x] Adaptive Jetpack Compose phone/tablet workspace; 1080dp wide-window instrumentation verifies side-by-side properties and composition viewer.
- [x] Offline starter project, app-private draft persistence, saved-project catalog, stable-ID save/upsert/deduplication and open/reload of latest revision.
- [x] Layer select/add/delete/reorder/rename, visibility and opacity; selection continuity and last-layer invariant.
- [x] Bounded 50-snapshot Undo/Redo for scoped project/layer/composition changes; deletion Undo/Redo restores project, selection and playhead.
- [x] Composition width/height/FPS/duration editing with validation, history and playhead clamping.
- [x] Timeline selection, frame scrubbing/timecode, FPS-aware playback, play/pause and accessible semantics.
- [x] Offline draft schema v1 round-trip/backward-compatible visibility default; invalid models rejected.
- [x] All 19 current emulator cases passed (18 EditorSmokeTest + 1 adaptive workspace), including `savedOfflineProjectsCanBeOpenedWithoutNetwork`, `effectCraftEcprojxXmlRoundTripsTypedProjectValues`, `deletingLayerUndoAndRedoRestoreTheExpectedTimelineSelection`, `editorSaveButtonPersistsTheCurrentOfflineLayerStack`, `nativeBridgeLoadsAndInvokesCanonicalParserOnEmulator`, and adaptive workspace.
- **Scope boundary:** bootstrap `.efx-draft` is an Android editing model, not upstream `.ecproj`; canonical engine-backed editing/preview remains M2/M3/M4. No mock render is represented as actual output.
- **Exit gate: SATISFIED for M1 foundation.** M2 onward continues independently.

## M3 — Actual preview/render engine
**Status: PARTIAL — actual upstream CPU compositor/JNI/session lifecycle and GPU path are implemented; CI #655 containing head passes its 92 API30 tests. CPU `comp_frame` cannot be preempted mid-call; physical ARM64 GPU/Surface/device-loss and fallback acceptance remain open.
- [x] Pin upstream `effectcraft-render` at the same immutable revision; native `render_frame_rgba` parses canonical `.ecproj`, renders through the real CPU `Renderer`/persistent `MediaPool`, and emits bounded RGBA with dimensions. Rust test asserts a 320×180 composition produces a real 160×90 frame payload and rejects bad IDs/negative frames (`b75fe05`, `4f0aa79`).
- [x] JNI frame request/output bridge and Kotlin wrapper (`4f0aa79`, `5fa1cf2`).
- [x] Imported canonical editor now renders frames off the UI thread, displays returned pixels as an image, and scrubs frame index (`05e2c4a`, `4c86391`). This is actual upstream CPU compositing—not the decorative draft viewer.
- [x] Main workspace viewer now requests the current imported canonical composition frame through JNI on `Dispatchers.Default`, decodes RGBA and displays the actual upstream CPU renderer output; scrubbing/playback refreshes the frame (`3b09f989`). The bootstrap `.efx-draft` remains non-renderable and is explicitly labelled `Draft preview not rendered` rather than faking output.
- [x] Main canonical workspace viewer is backed by one project/session/MediaPool handle; import/restore opens it, canonical edits update it before UI state, frame requests reuse it, and dispose closes it. The bootstrap `.efx-draft` remains honestly unrendered. Commit `d696c56`; exact-head CI #369 passed.
- [x] Add pinned upstream `effectcraft-media::MediaPool` as a persistent native `FootageSource`; renderer now decodes app-private relinked still/video/audio files through upstream FilmCraft codecs instead of `NoFootage` (`bc7ddc7`, `de7bce1`).
- [x] Host Rust test writes a PNG fixture to a local path and asserts its red pixels appear in the CPU composition frame (`b76e545`, `3da9fb9`); latest CI must validate cross-build and decode test.
- [ ] Verify media decoder feature/codec support and memory cache limits on Android ARM64; video/audio performance and cancellation are not yet measured.
- [x] JNI session lifecycle, native ownership/threading, cooperative cancellation, resize and teardown; Rust 21/21, JVM 61/61, emulator 22/22 on exact commit `d696c56`, CI #369 success. Cancellation cannot interrupt an upstream `comp_frame` already executing; it discards the result after the call.
- [ ] GPU/Vulkan acceleration plus tested CPU fallback/device-loss recovery.
- **Note (Run 37):** #10 stays PARTIAL. Run 41650672565 (head `f7d0ba3`) is containing-head evidence only for the GPU commits (`1d1d436`, `d5e1b78`, `2829b8a`); `sessionReportsBackendAndRecoversAfterSimulatedLoss` passed, but the actual backend value was not recorded, so GPU acceleration is NOT proven. A physical ARM64 device leg reporting backend gpu with a named adapter is still required; the Surface/Texture presentation path also remains open.
- [ ] Verify ARM64 native frame on physical phone/tablet; record device, ABI, SHA and pixel evidence.

## M5 — Render and export
**Status: IN PROGRESS — PNG/validated ZIP plus H.264 MediaCodec/MediaMuxer export are wired to settings/progress/cancel/SAF; staged MP4 worker validates before copy, AAC passthrough when compatible and explicit video-only reason otherwise. CI #655 on `36b9e4c9` passed 92/92 API30 tests including staged encode/validator/SAF copy-readback and Rust 42/42; #653's mdat parser failure was fixed in `36b9e4c9`. The v1 mvhd/mdhd field-width correction (`fdbe468d` + `c6c7f652`) is awaiting latest-head CI #661. Durable foreground export, physical codec/provider/audio sync, crop/letterbox and provider cancellation acceptance remain open.
- [x] SAF CreateDocument export of the current canonical composition/playhead frame as PNG, using a temporary full-resolution native session, bounded RGBA validation and correct pixel conversion; cancel before output write and show render/write/completion/failure status. Implementation commit `09c1f1b`; pixel fidelity and malformed/over-budget emulator tests.
- [x] Streaming ZIP sequence core: one frame at a time, PNG signature/IHDR/dimension validation, stable entry names/timestamps, progress and cooperative cancellation. Implementation `1910de56`, JVM tests `432f85ca`; green containing-head CI #380 on `f7d0ba3` verified 68/68 unit + 25/25 emulator cases.
- [x] `sequence.json` records schema/format, width/height/FPS, inclusive frame range and frame count; validator checks manifest CRC/size and plan equality before SAF copy (Run 42, latest CI pending).
- [x] Android SAF `CreateDocument(application/zip)` action runs full canonical composition range via native `NativeSession`, PNG-encodes each frame, stages under cache, copies only completed archive to user destination and cleans temp file; progress/cancel action. Commit `b9e3d4b5`; Compose callback test added; exact-head CI #383 pending.
- [x] User-controlled PNG-sequence width/height, integer FPS and inclusive frame range with RenderQueuePlan validation; defaults use composition dimensions/FPS/full range (commit `58d796df`, Compose assertion added). Width/height now resample actual native pixels through `RenderedFramePng.writeScaled` with a bounded source side (`65b3675` + `58d796df` + follow-up). Emulator scaling test added; latest CI pending. Codec/container templates remain open.
- [x] Staged ZIP manifest/frame/CRC validation before SAF copy and bounded SAF writer rollback on cancellation/error (Run 39/52; commits `e29b1356`, `deef8110`, `68346260`, `d4ac0db2`, `81656806`, `7fd56189`). Provider deletion is best-effort; some SAF providers may retain partial documents.

Provider-side partials cannot be universally rolled back, and universal rollback is not claimed. ### Run — M2 asset-manifest / repository / SAF-lifecycle cluster (2026-11-03)
- Base: `6d34c056d59d84c3245c13c1fc9f3b9665952b35`. Worked from a fresh clone; the stale `repo/` checkout was left untouched. Branch tip was re-checked with `gh` immediately before pushing and had not moved.
- Scope, one cluster because the pieces share one store, one manifest and one referenced-set: criteria #15 (asset library browse/replace/remove UX, project-to-asset manifest/garbage collection, missing-media recovery, SAF permission lifecycle), #28 (SAF write permission/recovery UX and editable canonical project repository adapter), #29 (canonical project repository adapter; version/schema validation and migration), #31 (durable asset manifest, deduplicated storage, integrity hashes, relink rollback/cleanup, sequence import). M2 checklist bullets are NOT flipped by this entry: CI on the exact head must pass first.
- Dedupe note (#21/#30): both overlapping tracker bullets describe SAF relink copy + path/missing-flag persistence, which is already implemented and checked in the existing SAF relink bullet. That existing bullet is treated as canonical; neither #21 nor #30 was implemented a second time and no duplicate bullet was added.
- New pure (JVM-testable, no Android dependency) data layer:
  - `ProjectAssetManifest` + `ProjectAssetManifestStore`: per-repository-project manifest (`format effectcraft-android-asset-manifest`, version 1), single entries (item id → SHA-256) and sequence entries (ordered, uniquely named frames), missing flags, last-known paths, strict hash/id/name validation, atomic JSON persistence under a SHA-256(project id) filename, `replaceAsset` across one manifest and `replaceAssetEverywhere` across all projects, union `allReferencedHashes`.
  - `CanonicalProjectRepository`: multi-project adapter beside the single-`latest.ecproj` cache. Document + versioned metadata sidecar per project; sidecar version is the schema version and document bytes are never mutated by versioning. Structural validation only (`.ecproj` must parse as a JSON object; `.ecprojx` must carry the `<EffectCraftProject>` root) — native schema validation remains the JNI import gate and XML normalization remains `CanonicalProjectDocument`. Legacy (version-less) sidecars migrate to v1 with defaults; newer-version sidecars are reported by `migrateAll`, never silently accepted; `update` rolls the document back if the sidecar write fails. `importLegacyDocument` is the migration path from the old single-document cache.
  - `SafPermissionRegistry`: file-backed record of persisted SAF grants (URI, READ/WRITE, display name, GRANTED/LOST). `reconcile` compares against the grants Android still reports: held grants recover to GRANTED, unheld grants become LOST (never deleted) so the UI can offer re-open recovery; only explicit release removes a record. The registry holds no document bytes or paths.
  - `SequenceAssetImport`: imports ordered frames through `OfflineAssetStore` (identical frames deduplicate to one payload) and records one sequence entry. On frame failure the manifest entry is restored and the failure rethrown, but already-imported payloads are NOT deleted — a hash may be referenced by another project, so only garbage collection may delete payloads.
  - `OfflineAssetStore` additions: `verify` (recomputed SHA-256 + size vs index), `verifyAll`, and `collectGarbage` (deletes unreferenced indexed assets, then content-addressed payload files with no index entry; failures are reported, never abort the sweep; a repeat run is a no-op). `OfflineAssetReferences.itemIdsFor` supports asset replacement over the legacy mapping.
- App wiring (`EffectCraftApp`, `EditorScreen`, both `EditorScreen` call sites updated — the two-call-site invariant from the tip fix still holds):
  - The referenced set is now computed in exactly one place as the union of all manifests, legacy references, and payload hashes the canonical document's own footage paths still point at. The last set is what makes library Replace safe: after a manifest replace, the old payload survives until the item is relinked and the native path moves, and only then may GC remove it.
  - Import records a READ grant only when `takePersistableUriPermission` succeeds, creates a repository entry, and clears legacy references; export records a WRITE grant the same way. Launch reconciles the cache against the repository (a cached document with no repository entry is imported, which also migrates pre-repository installs), runs `migrateAll`, reconciles SAF grants against `persistedUriPermissions`, and refreshes references.
  - Relink rollback chain, in order: manifest link → legacy reference → canonical cache → repository update; any failure restores every earlier step (including restoring the previous cache bytes when the repository update fails). Canonical edits update the repository entry with cache rollback on failure.
  - Media library dialog gains: a missing-media banner (manifest missing count), per-linked-asset Replace (picker → manifest + legacy replacement, with status copy stating the canonical-sheet relink still needed to move the native path), a Remove-unused-media action (garbage collection with deleted/orphan/failed counts in the status message), and a Storage-access section (grants with GRANTED/LOST state, Release per grant, re-open recovery copy). Removal blocking now uses the multi-project union, and its dialog copy says "linked by a project" instead of "linked by the current canonical project".
- Tests added (all JVM, no weakened assertions): `ProjectAssetManifestTest` (link/sequence/replace/persist/multi-project/strict-rejection/round-trip), `CanonicalProjectRepositoryTest` (create/update/list/delete/validation/update-rollback/legacy-migration/future-version-reporting/legacy-import), `SafPermissionRegistryTest` (record/reconcile/recovery/release/invalid), `SequenceAssetImportTest` (ordered frames + dedupe, failure rollback with payload retention, pre-import rejection), `OfflineAssetStoreGcTest` (verify corruption detection, GC unreferenced + orphans + idempotence), plus `itemIdsFor` coverage in `OfflineAssetReferencesTest`. New tests: 5 + 5 + 2 + 3 + 2 + 1 = 18.
- Format boundaries unchanged: `.efx-draft` stays private Android JSON, `.ecproj` JSON + `.ecprojx` XML supported, Effekseer `.efkproj` not implemented, Adobe `.aep` not promised without a legally available parser route. No `.aep` compatibility is claimed.
- Local verification limits (stated plainly, not green): no Android SDK platform-37 locally, `sdkmanager` cannot reach its repository from this sandbox, no local Kotlin toolchain or cached `org.json` jar was available, and no emulator exists — so no local Gradle/JVM run was possible. CI on the pushed head is the compile, JVM-test and instrumentation verifier for this change.
- Still to verify after CI: exact-head CI green (Kotlin compile incl. `:app:compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin`, JVM suite, 19/19 emulator instrumentation); physical ARM64 device leg (SAF persisted-permission behaviour, relink/replace/GC on a real device) is NOT part of this task and remains open; a repository picker UI (browsing/switching repository entries in the editor) is not built — the adapter plus import/edit persistence is the delivered #28/#29 scope, and the picker is follow-up work.

### Run 35 — bounded M5 frame-sequence planning (2026-11-03)
- Added pure Kotlin `RenderQueuePlan`/`RenderQueueSettings` for a future native-rendered PNG sequence ZIP executor. It preserves width/height/FPS and inclusive start/end frames, iterates lazily, produces deterministic six-digit PNG entry names, and computes bounded progress without retaining a frame list.
- Validation rejects invalid dimensions (1–4096), FPS outside 1–120, negative/reversed/out-of-range frames, queues over 10,000 frames, more than 8,388,608 pixels/frame, or >8 GiB estimated uncompressed RGBA. Output format is explicitly PNG sequence ZIP only; no video codec/audio capability is claimed.
- Four JVM test methods cover inclusive frame mapping/names, progress boundaries, invalid inputs, UHD budget and queue-budget rejection. Files: `app/src/main/java/ai/effectcraft/android/data/RenderQueuePlan.kt`, `app/src/test/java/ai/effectcraft/android/data/RenderQueuePlanTest.kt`.
- This is M5 planning/validation groundwork, not an executor or UI feature; single-frame PNG remains the only shipped export action. Exact-head CI will validate the new Kotlin/JUnit files.
- Concurrent CI #374 (head `2829b8a`) is running on the compile-fix commit. Its predecessor #373 failed at APK assembly solely on the already-diagnosed `importedEcprojx` typo; correction is now committed. The new queue-plan commit will supersede #374 and require a fresh exact-head run.


### Run 36 — M5 streaming PNG-sequence ZIP executor (2026-11-03)
- Implemented `PngSequenceZipExporter` over `RenderQueuePlan`: lazily renders one frame at a time, validates each PNG signature/IHDR and exact plan dimensions, writes deterministic ZIP entries with stable timestamps, reports completed-frame/percentage progress, and checks cancellation before render and before entry write. No decoded bitmap sequence is retained.
- Added 3 JVM test methods: ZIP order and byte-preservation/progress; cancellation prevents rendering the next frame and leaves the first entry readable (caller must discard partial SAF document); invalid PNG and dimension mismatch rejection.
- Implementation commit `1910de567aa3ae55727602a5ebf4c588e63969b6`; tests commit `432f85cad79be4aa895a9298f5481baf0494eed0`.
- Local Kotlin compile + executable smoke harness PASS: two ZIP entries with exact payload bytes, 50/100% progress, cancellation after frame 0, dimension mismatch rejected. This is standalone `kotlinc` verification; Android Gradle/JUnit CI remains authoritative.
- Exporter is a reusable streaming core, not yet connected to a SAF ZIP launcher/render queue UI. It writes directly to the supplied stream; cancellation leaves a partial ZIP, so the caller must abandon/delete the document or stage in app-private storage first. No video codec/audio capability is claimed.
- At start of run, CI #375 on the previous planner head was in progress after ARM64/x86_64 native builds, JNI, Rust tests and license inventory; APK/JVM/emulator pending. New commits supersede it; exact-head run for this executor must be inspected next.
- Next: wire executor to a CreateDocument(application/zip) UI using an app-private temporary ZIP (to make cancellation atomic), pass NativeSession render/PNG encoding per planned frame, expose width/height/FPS/range settings, then add end-to-end ZIP artifact validation and cancellation tests.


### Run 44 — offline media library aggregate quota and import rollback (2026-11-03)
- M6 offline robustness: OfflineAssetStore now enforces both the existing per-asset streaming cap (512 MiB default) and a configurable aggregate indexed-media cap (2 GiB default). Quota is checked after hashing but before publishing a new payload/index; identical SHA-256 payloads reuse their first metadata and do not count twice.
- New-payload publication is rolled back if index persistence fails, avoiding a dangling content-addressed file. The import temp file remains cleaned on quota rejection.
- Added two JVM regression tests: aggregate quota rejects a second asset without publishing its payload/temp file; a duplicate payload at the exact quota is accepted and counted once. No existing tests/assertions were removed.
- Exact-head CI #392 was inspected: #391 failed in Rust lib tests because the red-pixel test helper omitted image::ImageEncoder; commit 2ff34c83 adds the trait import and reports local cargo test --offline --lib 28/28. #392 is running on that fix head; this quota change will trigger #393 and requires its own exact-head JVM/emulator evidence.
- Local execution limitation: this automation sandbox has no Android SDK 37/emulator or local Kotlin/Gradle dependency cache, so only repository CI can establish Android/JUnit acceptance. The quota logic is deterministic and is covered by new JVM tests.


### Run 45 — validate PNG sequence frame range against canonical composition
- M5 correctness: frame-range validation now checks the inclusive end frame against the selected canonical composition's actual duration before launching SAF CreateDocument, and rechecks immediately before rendering. Previously a user-entered out-of-duration frame could create an empty output document before the native renderer rejected it.
- Added a composition-aware RenderQueuePlan overload and regression for zero-frame compositions, endFrame == durationFrames, and the last valid frame. Generic planner remains format-independent.
- Exact-head CI #393/#394 required for Kotlin/JUnit/emulator acceptance.

Unknown/future timestamps and recent files are retained; unrelated files and directories are never touched. Failed deletions are reported for later retry.

Android JUnit remains pending in CI #404.

### Run 48 — recover interrupted atomic draft saves (2026-11-03)
- M6 offline robustness: DraftProjectStore now reconciles store-owned *.efx-draft.tmp files on save/catalog load. It validates temp JSON and project identity against the target filename; a complete newer temp is atomically promoted (with replace fallback) when the committed target is absent, corrupt, or older. Incomplete/mismatched/superseded temps are removed while the last valid target is preserved. This recovers the latest fully serialized draft after process death between temp write and rename.
- Added Android instrumentation draftStoreRecoversNewerCompleteAtomicTempAfterProcessDeath: it creates a valid newer temp beside a committed baseline, calls store load, and asserts recovered content plus temp removal. Fixture timestamps derive from the persisted target to avoid clock assumptions.
- Source commit 4fadb5a5; test commit b5b7a3ec; timestamp-fixture correction 0e6d46e0. No tests/assertions were weakened.
- CI investigation: run #411 failed exactly one emulator test because Kotlin inferred the callback frame list as List<Long> while the callback supplied Int; commit 00fbe747 preserves the frame type. Its separate deterministic second-build comparison was still in progress at last inspection, so run #412 is not yet fully green/reproducibility accepted. New recovery commits trigger #413–#415; latest exact-head #415 is pending.

### Run 52 — SAF export cancellation and partial-document rollback (2026-11-04)
- Added `SafDocumentWriter` ([code](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/e29b135654100a4b9bb45cd52fb9ccec8b517ca4), [tests](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/deef8110c87e7c798400949ec371281dde7660d2)). Copies app-private artifacts to SAF in bounded 64 KiB chunks, checks cancellation between chunks, flushes only after completion, reports copied bytes, caps configurable buffers at 1 MiB, and invokes destination rollback on cancellation or any source/output failure (preserving the original exception and suppressing rollback errors).
- Integrated the helper into both full-frame PNG and sequence ZIP output ([integration](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/683462607fa817f60c3a0c151f91c322285abd56)). Frame PNG is now encoded into app-private staging before SAF copy; both paths attempt `DocumentsContract.deleteDocument` for a failed/cancelled newly-created destination. Provider deletion is best-effort (some SAF providers may reject delete); the failure is attached as suppressed and never hides the original export error.
- Added 4 JVM tests: bounded multi-chunk byte equality/progress; cancellation after partial output invokes rollback; provider write exception remains primary when rollback also fails; zero and >1 MiB buffers rejected before opening output. Assertions are additive. Exact-head CI has not completed yet.
- CI audit: [#421](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/actions/runs/41688924382) succeeded on containing head `7ca5ef36`: Rust 28/28, Gradle build/unit steps successful, emulator 29/29 (0 skipped/failed), reproducibility job successful. ### Run 68 — independent incoming/outgoing transform interpolation (2026-11-04)
- M4: native canonical transform interpolation now edits incoming and outgoing sides independently. Easy Ease copies the upstream eased tangent only for the selected side.

### Run 79 — stabilize curve selector regression and align Kotlin/native transform whitelist (2026-11-04)

### Run 86 — bounded native RGBA to YUV420 video-frame conversion (2026-11-04)

### Run 87 — H.264 MP4 streaming export pipeline (2026-11-04)
- M5 moved beyond conversion groundwork: `RenderOutputFormat.MP4_H264` and bounded even-dimension/bitrate validation (250 kbps–50 Mbps) are part of `RenderQueuePlan`; `Mp4SequenceExporter` selects an Android flexible-YUV420 H.264 encoder, renders one native RGBA frame at a time, scales/composites/converts it to I420, fills codec input Image planes using row/pixel strides, drains encoded buffers into `MediaMuxer` MP4, sends frame PTS from the requested FPS, emits EOS, and checks cancellation during render/input/output waits. The caller owns SAF FileDescriptor and must delete partial output on cancellation/failure; no full sequence is retained.
- Added JVM plan tests for even YUV420 dimensions and bitrate bounds, and moved Bitmap conversion tests to Android instrumentation (JVM Android SDK stubs cannot execute Bitmap methods). Instrumented tests verify red/white BT.601 values and output scaling. Converter commits de4c7b7f/815873ce; exporter commit [98c7e75c](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/98c7e75c8d9a67380b2a4cf5e84e7b68fd660c02); plan test [26ebaacb](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/26ebaacbd9e2108e248da8e770923c94457b0db7); instrumentation commits df164779 (created then removed from local JVM source set) and [f0be947c](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/f0be947c17b27641881f10eda621320978abf276). The invalid local Bitmap test was removed; its instrumented replacement is retained.
- MP4 encoder/muxer has not yet been exercised on emulator or physical hardware and is not wired into the editor's export picker; exact-head CI must validate compile/JVM/instrumentation. MediaCodec capability absence is an explicit runtime error (no silent fake output); audio muxing, background durable queue and output playback validation remain open.
- CI #600 (run 41851743879, 67bdf57d) addresses #599's two layer visibility failures by moving Surface preview below layer list; it remains on an older source while export commits landed. Latest doc/source workflow [#?] is pending; inspect branch runs for exact HEAD before accepting. No local Android/Rust toolchain in this environment, no test pass claimed. ### Run 94 — validate finalized MP4 output before reporting export success (2026-11-05)
- M5 acceptance gap found after inspecting the current app flow: MediaCodec/MediaMuxer returned an encoder frame count/duration, but the production SAF callback set `outputCompleted=true` without reopening the finalized container. A successful encoder return alone did not prove the destination was readable, had requested dimensions, or contained the planned video samples.
- Added `Mp4OutputValidator`: it opens finalized SAF Uri (or app-private file in instrumentation) with MediaExtractor, requires H.264 track and exact dimensions, reads every sample with strictly increasing PTS beginning at zero, requires exact planned sample count, and checks track duration against frameCount/FPS within one frame. It uses a bounded sample buffer independent of queue length. App now validates after closing the output descriptor and reports 100%/success only after the validator agrees with the encoder; any validation error follows existing rollback and deletes the invalid SAF document.
- Extended the real emulator encode/demux regression to assert validator dimensions, frame count, duration and MIME in addition to reading encoded samples. Prior exact-head CI #649 Assemble/test job passed on `3ae559ad`: Rust 42/42, JVM 200/200, API 30 instrumentation 89/89 (0 failures/skips), including large-project benchmark, SAF import/relink/export round-trip and real H.264 MP4 encode/demux. Its independent cold APK rebuild is still running; this code commit intentionally supersedes that cold job. Fresh exact-head CI must verify validator and reproducibility; no validator pass claimed before it.
- M5 remains IN PROGRESS: audio, physical codec matrix, SAF provider diversity, durable background queue and export recovery remain open. ### Run 113 — verify M0 cold reproducibility and close repository update crash window (2026-11-05)

- Code inspection found a real M6 loss/corruption window in `CanonicalProjectRepository.update`: process death after the new document was atomically promoted but before the SHA-256 sidecar promotion left the old sidecar digest pointing to new bytes; `metadata()` then rejected the project and the picker hid it. Existing exception rollback did not cover process death.

### Automation execution 12 — repository rollback recovery regression fixed (2026-11-05)
- Inspected PR #1, branch head, Actions #765–#768 and exact #767 logs. #765 SUCCESS on old head d1660eb7 (42 Rust, 264 JVM, 132 API30 connected; cold APK/native reproducibility). #767 FAILED on 249aefbb: ARM64/x86_64 JNI, Rust and APK assembly succeeded; unit tests had 268 tests with one failure: CanonicalProjectRepositoryTest.interruptedDocumentPromotionRollsBackToDurableProjectPairOnFreshLoad at line 66. The assertion expected the original document after recovery.
- Root cause: backup-pair verification called parseSidecar(), whose default integrity check compares its declared digest to the currently promoted canonical document. During interrupted promotion that is the new document, so a valid old backup sidecar was rejected against the wrong file; rollback never ran. This was a production recovery defect surfaced by a regression test, not a flaky test.
- Fix [d31daf6f](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/d31daf6fa9e7f6b425e9d546faca37a96ca1c743): parse rollback metadata with document-integrity comparison disabled only for the backup sidecar, then explicitly validate backup document bytes against that sidecar digest. Normal canonical metadata integrity validation remains enabled.
- Hardening [4dd8536c](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/4dd8536c824bb752365f32b4be48cfdd47f19df2): recovery now copies from durable backup files into fsynced restore temporaries and atomically promotes both; it never consumes the backup pair until the restored canonical pair has been verified. This keeps the rollback source intact if recovery itself is interrupted between file promotions.
- CI #768 (run 42000414079, head d31daf6f) was in progress when hardening was pushed and is superseded. Exact-head CI for 4dd8536c and the tracker update is required; no new test pass is claimed. Automation runtime has no local Gradle/JDK/Android SDK/Cargo toolchain.
- M0 reproducibility remains demonstrated at #765 but exact-head revalidation pending. M1 scoped editor accepted. M2–M7 emulator gates are bounded and physical/provider/full-parity/release items remain open. M0 owner sign-off stays open; PR #1 draft/unmerged. No signing secrets, release, merge, Adobe .aep parser, or unsupported .efkproj compatibility claim.


### Run 114 follow-up — recovery parser and rollback durability (2026-11-05)
- CI #767 exact log confirmed the single failing assertion was the interrupted document-promotion recovery test (268 JVM tests, 1 failure; native builds/Rust/APK passed). The failing line expected the previous canonical JSON after a fresh repository load.
- Root fix [d31daf6f](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/d31daf6fa9e7f6b425e9d546faca37a96ca1c743) adds a narrowly scoped verifyDocumentIntegrity=false mode only when parsing a rollback sidecar, because its digest must be compared to the backup document, not the currently promoted canonical document. Backup bytes are explicitly checked against the declared SHA-256; normal live sidecar integrity remains strict.
- Recovery hardening [4dd8536c](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/4dd8536c824bb752365f32b4be48cfdd47f19df2) writes fsynced restore temp files, atomically promotes them, verifies the resulting canonical pair, and retains rollback files until verification. This avoids consuming the only backup during an interrupted recovery.
- Legacy migration edge fixed [ea55290c](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/ea55290c28ecda804e4d7095fd11bf51f30e38fa): update's durable rollback sidecar now materializes the digest derived by metadata() when the original v0 sidecar lacked documentSha256; otherwise backup verification could not prove the old document after process death.

- While adding the Android `.ecprojx` round-trip test, found the parser required the very next pull-parser event after the root END_TAG to be END_DOCUMENT. Valid XML formatting whitespace (and comments) after the root could be rejected as “Unexpected content”. Updated `CanonicalProjectDocument.parseXmlProject` to consume legal whitespace/comments and still reject non-whitespace text or a second root. Added API30 regression that appends whitespace/comment to serializer output and verifies canonical data survives. This is parser compatibility hardening; it does not relax schema/root/version checks.
- Production parser + API30 regression committed [4b077ef3](https://github.com/sunmughan/After-Effects-Android/commit/4b077ef301ddfb29e8def5ee50f743d7defe2bae). CI #864 is in progress on prior head and will be superseded; exact-head instrumentation required. **Release NOT COMPLETE.**

### Engineering Run 11 — M4 canonical ShapePath editing (2026-11-06)
- Added native metadata for `ParamUi::Path` properties under shape/mask/effect groups. Canonical path metadata carries the complete upstream ShapePath (vertices, in/out tangents, closed state and feather points) plus sorted key frame indices.
- Implemented `set_layer_path_property` and JNI/Kotlin bridge. It accepts upstream ShapePath JSON, bounds vertex count to 1..4096, rejects non-finite vertices/tangents, excessive tangent arrays and invalid feather segment/t/radius/tension values, normalizes missing tangent arrays, verifies the target UID is a Path property and commits through canonical project serialization.
- Added Android inspector path JSON editor (composition-coordinate vertices/tangents) and Apply path action for both shape-item and effect groups; EffectCraftApp routes edits through existing canonical save/history/session update. This is a first functional path-editing surface, not a visual Bezier vertex/handle tool; animated path keyframes are not exposed here.
- Native regression builds a real mask ShapePath, checks metadata, updates geometry/closed state and rejects NaN/empty paths/invalid feather/unknown UID. JVM metadata regression parses ShapePath JSON and key frames. API30 instrumentation grafts a mask into a real canonical project, edits via JNI, verifies re-inspection and that an open NativeSession accepts/renders the edited project. Commits: `615cc556` native; `5dca8e0c` metadata; `12c51fa8` bridge; `ad25307a` inspector; `6458faef` label correction; `acc76420` canonical persistence; `cc9bb583` JVM test; `353c3758` API30 test.
